# Ender-3 "R2-D2" - complete Klipper macro system (ENGLISH)

For your modded Ender-3 (linear rails, dual Z in parallel, SKR Mini E3 V3.0,
direct drive with BMG, BTT filament sensor, MicroProbe,
Creality 12864 LCD, Mainsail) - **with feedback about EVERYTHING**: R2-D2 beeper
sounds + LCD + Mainsail console at once.

> This is the ENGLISH rewrite: all macros, menus, messages and comments use
> standard community naming. It replaces the earlier German version completely.

---

## What you get

| Feature | Where |
|---|---|
| **Asked before every print**: print as sliced OR with changes (material, temps 1st/following layers, retract, PA, accel, outer wall/support speed+accel, ironing flow/speed/temp, flow) | LCD "Start Print?" + "Pre-Print" / Mainsail `PRINT_AS_SLICED` / `PRINT_WITH_OVERRIDES` |
| **Old gcode files just work**: a state watcher detects prints that never call PRINT_START -> overrides + live tuning activate anyway; M104/M109/M140/M190/M204 are intercepted and redirected | automatic |
| **Everything LIVE during the print**: speed & accel separately for outer wall/inner wall/infill/support/ironing, flow, PA, retract, ironing (flow/temp/speed), fan scaling, temperatures, babystepping | LCD "Live Tuning" / Mainsail |
| **Dynamic print start**: reads the file's temps, loads the material's mesh or re-levels (adaptive!), PID deviation check based on the steady print temperature | automatic (08_) |
| **Material presets** (PLA/PETG/ABS/ASA/TPU): bed + hotend (layer 1 / from layer 2) + PA + flow + retraction - LCD-editable, saveable, loadable into the pre-print overrides | LCD "Pre-Print > Material" |
| **PA calibration (Orca style)**: one long line per PA value - speeds, accel, spacing, line length all on the LCD; prime line + skirt; calculator (best line or direct value -> PA saved) | LCD "Maintenance > PA Cal." |
| **Cold pull nozzle cleaning** + filament load/unload/change at the right temperatures (print temp while printing, min 200C in idle) | LCD "Maintenance > Filament" |
| **Input shaper calibration** (accelerometer): freq range, max smoothing, auto-save, raw-data dump - full guide in ISHAPER.md | LCD "Maintenance > Vibration Cal." |
| **Mesh management**: save with the bed temperature as name, live list of existing meshes, load + keep active for prints, unload to re-level next print | LCD "Maintenance > Bed Mesh" |
| **Z babystepping in 0.04 mm full steps** ("magic 4" - matches the Ender-3 Z motor step) | LCD "Live Tuning" + probe calibration |
| **Mesh persistence is YOUR choice**: default session-only (no SAVE_CONFIG after prints) - toggle "Save after print" to automate | LCD "Bed Mesh" menus |
| **Safe pause**: lifts only up to Z-max, park corner far away from the objects (object data!), anti-ooze retract, sensor muted, idle timeout infinite; **quick pause NOW** or **gentle pause at next layer / layer N** | `PAUSE` / `QUICK_PAUSE` / `PAUSE_NEXT_LAYER` / `PAUSE_AT_LAYER` |
| **Filament change without abort** (M600, multi-color): park, unload, purge, continue | `CHANGE_FILAMENT` / `M600` |
| **Filament runout**: R2 ALARM (red strobe + siren), safe pause, no wasted filament | automatic |
| **Cancel protection**: first press pauses safely, second press confirms, RESUME disarms - a mis-press can never abort a print | `CANCEL_PRINT` |
| **Skip single objects**: failed part? Exclude it right at the display and save the rest of the print (2-press confirmation, last-object protection, works while paused too) | LCD "Live Tuning > Objects" / `SKIP_OBJECT` / `SKIP_CURRENT_OBJECT` / `SHOW_OBJECTS` |
| **Dual-Z gantry sync** by gently bumping the top stop (reduced current) - manual, after boot or before prints | `SYNC_GANTRY` |
| **Maintenance/calibration**: PID (hotend WITH fan!), bed mesh, bed screws (SCREWS_TILT), probe offset, extruder calibration, retract test, self test | LCD "Maintenance" |
| **System on the LCD**: FW restart, host restart, shutdown, save config (each double-press) | LCD "System" |
| **KAMP compatible**: detects installed KAMP automatically - adaptive mesh via KAMP, LINE/VORON purge, optional Smart Park (each toggleable, fallback to native ADAPTIVE / own prime line) | LCD "Maintenance > KAMP" |
| **Old files fully automatic**: pause at start -> bed to temp -> mesh -> prime line -> continue; falling temps in the file are detected as 'layer 2' | LCD Pre-Print > Bed Mesh > 'Level old files' |
| **Power off from the LCD**: heaters safe + Pi shutdown (needs gcode-shell-command, 2-press) | `SYS_SHUTDOWN` |
| **Manual PID tuning**: hotend (WITH part fan) + bed at any temperature, anytime - no material binding, no automatic tuning | LCD Maintenance > PID Tuning / `TUNE_PID` |
| **Generators on the LCD**: Range, Labels, Plotter, Cable ties, Angles+Flat, Lithophanes, ColorPic, Gaskets, Frames, Bricks/Kits/Figures — and **String Art** (R178): a photo becomes strands woven over an anchor ring, SPIRAL needs no image | LCD "Tools" |
| **Boot self-check**: console confirms `Macro system OK: all modules loaded`; a missing include OR an outdated file copy is reported loudly with the exact filename | `_CHECK_INSTALL` |
| **R2-D2**: 19 sounds woven into every flow | automatic |

---

## Files

```
klipper-e3-r2d2/
├── printer.cfg.example         reference config (SKR Mini E3 V3.0 pins) + include list
├── macros/
│   ├── 01_state.cfg            persistent + volatile state, defaults, STATE WATCHER
│   ├── 02_sounds.cfg           M300 (official Klipper sample) + R2-D2 sound library
│   ├── 04_notify.cfg           NOTIFY = LCD+Mainsail+sound / SHOW_STATUS
│   ├── 05_materials.cfg        material presets + SET_MATERIAL
│   ├── 06_temp_controls.cfg    M104/M109/M140/M190/M106/M204 interception + LAYER_MARK
│   ├── 07_start_confirm.cfg    start dialog poller + override setters/toggles
│   ├── 08_print_start_stop.cfg PRINT_START/PRINT_END, mesh+PID decisions, prime line
│   ├── 09_pause_filament.cfg   PAUSE/PARK/RESUME/CANCEL/M600/runout
│   ├── 10_live_tuning.cfg      APPLY_TYPE + all live SET_* controllers
│   ├── 11_gantry_sync.cfg      dual-Z synchronization
│   ├── 12_maintenance.cfg      PID/mesh/screws/offsets/extruder/belt/self test
│   ├── 13_system.cfg           restarts/shutdown/info
│   ├── 14_kamp.cfg             KAMP compatibility (mesh/purge/smart park dispatch)
│   ├── 15_objects.cfg          skip single objects (SKIP_OBJECT, 2-press confirm)
│   ├── 16_labels.cfg           label generator (multi-plate, GRIFF, stamps)
│   ├── 17_generator.cfg       all 19 generators + texture LIBRARY (95 presets)
│   ├── 18_colorpic.cfg         color-picture generator (M600 swaps, purge tower)
│   └── 19_bricks.cfg           bricks / kits / figures generator
├── menus/lcd_menues.cfg        the complete English LCD menu tree (12864)
├── slicer/
│   ├── ORCA.md / CURA.md       step-by-step slicer setup
│   ├── orca_klipper_features.py    Orca/Prusa post-processing (APPLY_TYPE)
│   └── cura_KlipperFeatureTypes.py Cura script (APPLY_TYPE+LAYER_MARK+object box)
└── tools/
    ├── config_validator.py     validates all templates/variables (python3 tools/config_validator.py)
    ├── macro_render_test.py    30+ logic tests with a mocked printer
    ├── r164_serial_budget.py   MCU serial-budget audit: worst move rate of every
                                file class vs the 450/s 'Timer too close' budget
                                at 100/125/150% live speed
    ├── r166_template_check.py  boot-blocker firewall: replays Klipper's EXACT
    │                           config pipeline (#-strip, configparser, single-
    │                           brace jinja compile) over every macro template
    └── r167_aircheck.py        geometry-reality audit: measures the generated
                                backplate/shaft FIT from the gcode (0.15-0.6mm
                                play/side), scans for floating extrusions
                                (45-deg support rule, bridges exempt) and the
                                M204 thrift of generated files
```

See **INSTALL.md** for setup in ~10 minutes and **MANUAL.md** for the complete
operating manual (every LCD item, every macro, every automatic event explained). (including migration from the old
German version!).

---

## The most important behaviors (by design)

1. **PID tuning is manual**: Maintenance > PID Tuning, any temperature, anytime
   (hotend with part fan, then SAVE_CONFIG - Klipper restarts once). No automatic
   tuning in the print start anymore.
2. **ONE bed mesh profile `r2`**: re-measured every print by default - adaptive
   (KAMP) when the file has object data. "Re-mesh every: AUTO" instead loads the
   saved r2 mesh. Materials no longer carry meshes.
3. **Old files without markers**: the setters apply the speed factor IMMEDIATELY
   via M220 when no feature type is known - you always get instant feedback.
   With the post-processing script the change is applied per feature boundary.
4. **Live retract** requires firmware retraction (Orca checkbox / Cura
   experimental) - otherwise the retract override only applies at print start.
5. **"PRINT_AS_SLICED" = truly 1:1**: this decision bypasses ALL overrides for
   exactly this print (including the M104/M109 interceptors). "PRINT_WITH_OVERRIDES"
   applies overrides + material preset + the matching mesh.
6. **Mainsail = LCD**: every menu action is a macro with a speaking English name
   and description -> identical in Mainsail. The start dialog also accepts
   Mainsail's resume button as "print as sliced".
7. **KAMP**: support detects automatically what is installed (setup: INSTALL.md
   section 3b). Mesh results are additionally saved as material profiles - the
   material logic survives KAMP.
9. **Comments & braces in macros** (important for your own edits!): Klipper cuts
   every line at the first `#` - comments always on their OWN line, never
   `{# ... #}` inline. Expressions use single braces `{...}` - `{{...}}` is
   invalid in Klipper. Booleans are Python style `True`/`False` (never `true`).
   `tools/config_validator.py` checks all of this.

## Safety notes

- Stay and watch the first `SYNC_GANTRY` run (documented in 11_gantry_sync.cfg).
- MicroProbe `x_offset/y_offset/z_offset` and the `[screws_tilt_adjust]` positions
  must be adapted once to your setup (marked ADJUST).
- Test the runout once: pull the filament out of the sensor during a print ->
  it must alarm + park safely (never cancel!).

| Version | Highlights |
|---|---|

## Latest additions

| Version | Highlights |
|---|---|
| **R2-2026-11-26.11 (R290)** | **Config-parse fix + texture engine for normal 3D objects + documentation/PDF truth-check.** (1) **_INIT_DEFAULTS config-parse fix (release-blocking):** the `{% if sv.nozzle_mode ... %}SAVE_VARIABLE ...` line in `macros/01_state.cfg` had lost its `gcode:`-block indentation, so Klipper parsed it as a config option (`Option '... save_variable variable' is not valid in section 'gcode_macro _init_defaults'`) and blocked Connect; indentation restored. A systematic scan of all `macros/*.cfg` confirmed this was the ONLY occurrence. (2) **TEXTURE MAPPING FOR NORMAL 3D OBJECTS (R290, not vase-mode-only):** verified that the texture engine also maps imported G-code and ordinary 3D objects (multi-wall + infill + top/bottom, non-vase) — multi-object files get per-object centers from `EXCLUDE_OBJECT_DEFINE CENTER`, each object's own circumference/height/loop-lengths/pattern, with per-object context swap in pass 2 and approach-vector reset; solo-object files stay byte-identical. Variable layer height handled via absolute Z. (3) **r81 re-freeze (stamp-class drift):** the `POSTPROC_RELEASE` stamp changes the `; R2-GEN core ...` header by +2 bytes per mr file; the goldens were re-frozen from the current module — r81 **23/23** byte-identical again. (4) **Documentation & PDF truth-check:** all 28 PDFs rebuilt on the current version stamp (dynamic from `r2_version.py`); stale hard-coded pattern/preset counts in TEXTURING/MANUAL/GENERATORS/GEOMETRY/CREATIVE made DYNAMIC (55 patterns / 95 presets, read from `PAT_ORDER`/Library); `docs/TOOLS_INVENTORY.md` line counts corrected and R290/PIC/nozzle-clean added; `MANUAL.md`/`TESTANLEITUNG.md` stale counts fixed; README history brought up to date. |
| **R2-2026-11-19.3 (R285)** | **Casting molds on the printer, complete 28-PDF scientific library, M82 arc-extrusion parity & foreign-printer audit.** (1) **CASTING-MOLD GENERATOR (`J2`, R270–R273):** `make_mold()` + `make_mold_blank()` in pure stdlib (streaming per-Z-band silhouettes, zero `trimesh` on Pi Zero 2 W): 2-part equator split (`AUTO`/`MID`/`OFF`, both halves printed parting-face-up with Y-mirrored top lid so neither cavity bridges over air), 3- and 4-part sector molds (`sectors=1/2` with streaming slide/lift demold proofs, `<= 25 mm` bridge span guard and horizontal dowel tunnels), sprue + conical funnel + adaptive high-spot vents + cell-snapped registration dowels, cell-eroded draft angle (`0..3°`), `STANDARD` / `FINE` (`15 %`-flow parting-face ironing at `20 mm/s`) / `FUZZY` (outer-wall-only jitter, cavity untouched), parametric source-less blanks (`MEDALLION`, `PLAQUE`, `BAR`) with flush relief-seat boss (`pocket`), LCD `Tools > Casting molds`, macros `MOLD_INFO`/`MOLD_CREATE`/`MOLD_BLANK_INFO`/`MOLD_BLANK_CREATE`, and single-piece `mold`/`mold_blank` toolchain steps (`r270` 33/33, `r271` 25/25, `r272` 20/20, `r273` 22/22). Also closed a latent `GEN_SUBDIRS['stencil']` watcher lookup gap from R267, a Cake LCD `and 0 or` label bug, and two missing `]` tokens in `update.sh`'s R268 same-date version comparator (`--git` downgrade guard + multi-ZIP selector, caught by `r234` G6). (2) **FOREIGN-PRINTER & MACHINE-MODEL AUDIT (`J7`, R274):** synthetic multi-printer verification across `180..300 mm` beds, `0.4..0.8 mm` nozzles, positive/negative probe offsets and low-Z axes (`COMPATIBILITY.md` §9 + `r274_compat_check.py` 24/24). (3) **M82 ARC & SINGLE-AXIS EXTRUSION FIX (`R276`):** `apply_twist()` and `apply_mirror()` now track cumulative `M82` extrusion across `G2`/`G3` arcs, single-axis `G1` moves and selective `OBJ=<name>` KAMP `POLYGON=` headers (`ΣΔE = 0.00000 mm`, zero negative E jumps). (4) **SECTION D COMPLETE — ALL 28 SCIENTIFIC & HANDBOOK PDFS (`R275–R279`) + GESAMTBRIEFING §§ 20–28 CROSSWALK (`R280`):** all **13/13 individual generator monographs** (`MOLDS`, `STENCIL`, `LITHOPHANE`, `COLORPIC`, `STRINGART`, `BRICKS`, `LABELS`, `FRAMES`, `PLOTTER`, `ANGLES`, `CABLETIES`, `GASKETS`, `GEOMETRY.pdf`) and all **7/7 modifier mathematics & blind-operation monographs** (`TEXTURING`, `CUTSPLIT`, `SCALE`, `TWIST`, `MIRROR`, `RANGE`, `TOOLCHAIN.pdf`) built from live engine runs and visually verified per D6 (`r275` 15/15, `r276` 22/22, `r277` 22/22, `r278` 25/25, `r279` 25/25), plus paragraph-by-paragraph crosswalk `analyse/GESAMTBRIEFING-ABGLEICH-20-28.md`. Gates (all serial, this tree): macro_render, megatest, review_checks PASS, r81 23/23 (stamp-class re-freeze, A/B-proven against V5), r91, audit_sweep, r110, systemtest 214/0, r142/r143, r197, r212..r244, r238 67/0, r267 34/0, r270..r279, r165/r265 clean, r168 19/0, r218 26/26, toolchain 49+27, gcode_re 10/10, r199 at the built package. No printer/hardware acceptance claimed — offline gates only. |

(2) **DEEP RELIEF (R266):** E rides the real path - depth up to 4 mm (was capped by per-cell E), the wall follows the carved surface, per-feature speed/PA stay honest; TEXTURING.pdf regenerated (64 pages). (3) **SPRAY STENCILS (R267, J9):** the lithophane image list feeds a stencil generator - 2-4 tone bands, one plate per band (spray lightest first), bridges automatic at 3x nozzle (the O-center stays attached), rectangle plate + 8 mm frame + 4 registration holes D4.2 mm at the identical corner position (stack on M4 pins), optional negative stencil; honest band reduction (under 2% of the area = no hairline plates, the step count drops with a note). LCD Tools > Stencil, macros STENCIL_INFO/STENCIL_CREATE. (4) **THE UPDATE ODOMETER WAS DATE-BLIND (R268, found by the release gate - release-blocking):** the version scheme is date-based since V1 (R2-YYYY-MM-DD.n) and the counter RESETS with every new date, but both update gates (git path + zip path) compared only counter+suffix - V5 (R2-2026-11-19.1) looked OLDER than V4 (R2-2026-11-18.3): the --git update would have been refused and the zip gate would even have PREFERRED an old zip lying in the folder. Both gates compare DATE first now, then counter, then suffix (octal-proof); r110's fixture zips derive their date from the repo. (5) **The field-pin family repaired:** seven batteries (r215/r226/r234/r237/r240/r242/r244) had rotted invisibly since V4 (frozen .142 version pins, frozen section counts, a mock without _SDM, an unrealistic no-travel fixture, a stale queue-clear pin, PREV_VER hardcoded to the .142 date) - all green again, version/section pins are DYNAMIC now so they cannot rot per release. | **.142m (R257)** | **The probe descent is where it died - and the LEDs were talking into it.** The three DOCTOR packs from 12.11. carry **8 MCU shutdowns**; **6 of them** report the same toolhead position: `(115.0, 115.0, Z)` with Z = 4.76 / 5.13 / 8.67 / 9.91 / 10.0 / 10.0. `115,115` is the `[safe_z_home] home_xy_position` of the field `printer.cfg` and `z_hop = 10` - so those are not crashes during printing, they are crashes **while the probe was moving**, the one phase where the stepper queue runs at a 1 ms step interval (`queue_step interval=64000 count=50`). The send-queue dump of the same second shows what travelled next to it: `neopixel_send` three times in 12 ms, `tmcuart_send`, `st7920_send_data`; before it `Neopixel update did not succeed`, `bytes_retransmit=85`, `buffer_time=0.000` and a clock model 191 ppm off. One `SET_LED_STATE` change writes the strip **2-4 times back to back** (STOP_LED_EFFECTS + effect + nozzle + bedlight), the WS2812 output is bit-banged with interrupts off, and a failed transfer blocks the **host** until the serial retry timeout - in the probe window that is exactly one move too late. **Fix:** `_LED_QUIET` mutes the animation for the duration of the motion (one write before the first move, state restored after the last); `G28`, `_CALIBRATE_MESH_PROFILE`, `SCREWS_TILT_CALCULATE` and `PROBE_CALIBRATE` are bracketed, `SET_LED_STATE` only *records* the wanted state while muted, and `_STATE_WATCHER` heals a mute that was left behind by a motion that died. **Proof:** `r257_crash_guard_check.py` runs 15 checks and then deletes each guard in turn - all 7 mutations must fail, otherwise the run fails; the battery adds 7 function tests that render the macros and count the writes (`2100/2100`). Nothing was switched off, no service touched: the strip keeps showing its last colour. **Also in this release:** the `R253 -> R257` tree was committed as one (the `.142j` - `.142l` packages had been built from the working tree without a commit - that was my mistake, and from now on every package has a revert point). |
| **.142l (R256)** | **My own boot halt, fixed within the hour — plus the drift audit the user asked for.** (1) WHAT HAPPENED: R255 wrapped `G28` to mark every homing as `mode='homing'` and gave the built-in an alias `_G28_RAW`. Klipper refuses an alias of a DIFFERENT name type — `G-Code macro rename of different types ('G28' vs '_G28_RAW')` → `Printer is halted` at every boot. The prompt fix is one word: the alias is now **M99110**, exactly the pattern the temperature wrappers have used since R1 (M99104 for M104, ...). The guard itself is unchanged. (2) THE RULE IS NOW CHECKED, NOT REMEMBERED: `r166_template_check.py` walks every `[gcode_macro X]` with `rename_existing: Y` in the whole tree, refuses a pair whose name TYPES differ (traditional g-code = letter + digits) and refuses an alias claimed twice. Proven by putting `_G28_RAW` back — the check fails with the exact Klipper message quoted in it. (3) DRIFT AUDIT (`r256_drift_audit.py`, new): the field complaint "Pre-Print says A, Live says B" gets a static data-flow answer over all **2295 LCD rows** — every numeric read-out is mapped to the variable that feeds it. Result on the shipped tree: **335 shown variables, every one of them written somewhere, not a single name fed by two different macros** — the only name that lives in two macros is `total_layers`, and that is a deliberate fallback (`_PREVIEW` only answers while `_STATE.file_type` is unknown). The audit also catches the reverse defects for good: a row whose text names one quantity while reading another, a row that shows a value nothing writes, and a variable name declared by two macros. Three false positives were found and removed while building it (the regex once blamed `_MATERIALS` and `_SDM` for keys they do not even have; dynamically written names like `{gen}_preset_page` and the watcher's `shaper_best_<axis>` looked dead) — each one is documented in the tool, so the next run does not repeat the mistake. (4) The register (`docs/SHUTDOWN_REGISTER.md`) now has 15 crash sites, the new one being my own regression, with the lesson written down: a green Jinja compile does not prove that Klipper LOADS the config — since .142l both checks run statically. |
| **.142k (R255)** | **The homing crash — found in the user's own log, fixed at the root, and put in ONE register.** (1) WHAT THE LOG SAYS: three sessions, all three died at bed centre right after the second probe contact on the 1 cm lift (`toolhead (115,115,10)`). Around that moment: reactor stalls of 0.361 s and 0.633 s, all of them `_got_pipe_signal` + `process_received` (Moonraker requests being worked off inside klippy), **20 API requests still queued** when the MCU gave up, `buffer_time=0.000` in 63-70 % of the samples before it, `bytes_retransmit` 9 -> 13 and a **100-message receive queue** — while the MCU itself was idle (`mcu_awake=0.014`, `mcu_task_avg=0.000047`, far below 1 %). So: the HOST delivered the next move late, not the file, not the move rate. (2) OUR OWN CONTRIBUTION IN THAT WINDOW: two identical 4 KB `gcode/script` pushes at 597.375 s carrying `_PREVIEW_SET` + `_FILE_INFO` + the **whole EXCLUDE_OBJECT polygon** — while `G28` ran. (3) THE ROOT CAUSE, in our code: the start dialog homes with `_STATE.mode` = `idle`, so every "is the printer moving?" gate was blind exactly there; the selection-preview branch had **no gate at all**; and the two motion probes shared ONE `print_time` sample, so they cancelled each other out ("no delta = not moving") and the push went out during homing. (4) THE FIX, one layer: **G28 is wrapped** (`rename_existing: _G28_RAW`) — every homing, from any of the 22 call sites, marks `mode='homing'` while it runs, restores the old mode afterwards, and a delayed_gcode releases the guard if a G28 dies mid-way (zero disk writes). Every gate has its own sample slot **and** a 2 s motion hold (a probe dwell is still motion); the selection push defers to idle instead of firing blind; the object polygon is left to the file above 1200 chars (the file ships and runs the same line); the synchronous `SAVE_VARIABLE` (an SD-card write) moved out of the homing block it stood in. `_printer_busy` and the strict push gate (SD mirror / gen_files) know `homing` too. New gate `r255_homing_guard_check.py`: **18 checks**, including a functional test of the probe answer against a stubbed Moonraker. (5) THE REGISTER THE USER ASKED FOR: **`docs/SHUTDOWN_REGISTER.md`** — 14 crash sites in one table, each with its field evidence, its cause, its fix and the test that holds it, plus what was deliberately NOT touched (UART, `max_z_velocity`/`max_z_accel`, `samples: 2`, the stable post-processor). (6) HONESTY ABOUT THE FILE: `LampOpenE27.gcode` was NOT the cause and needed no fix — the crash happens before a single file move runs, which is also why the segment fix could never be reached. The `MOVERATE=5372` warning stays (it is a real risk for the print itself). (7) The `R2_DOCTOR` errors in the pasted console come from BEFORE the update (11:51); the three boots afterwards load `DOCTOR_PACK` — the rename holds. Two battery findings caught this round and were fixed before release: a `description:` line in a `[delayed_gcode]` section (the .120 boot-halt class) and a Jinja brace error in the new G28 body (the compat sweep renders it on a minimal printer). |
| **.142j (R254)** | **Our own g-code now says what it is - plus the two handbooks the user asked for.** (1) TYPE MARKERS, BOTH OF THEM: the generators only wrote `APPLY_TYPE` (fine for Klipper, invisible to every standard reader - which is exactly why the object STL export found 'no object extrusion' in a TYPE=MARKED file). One helper (`_apply_type_lines`) now emits the `;TYPE:<canonical name>` comment AND the command, used by all 46 write sites; 38 now-duplicate comment lines were removed with it, so nothing stands twice. Travel markers stay as they were - travel is not a print feature, no slicer tags it. (2) GOLDEN RE-FROZEN WITH PROOF: all 23 r81 cases were diffed old vs new - 55 added / 39 removed lines, and apart from the 26 version-stamp lines and the 26 re-computed R2-META signatures every single line of the delta is a `;TYPE:` comment (28 added, 12 removed). Not one motion line changed; pr01-pr10 stay byte-identical. (3) TWO STALE TEST EXPECTATIONS FIXED, not silenced: r197 counted prime-tower tags as literals only (they now come from the helper too - the check counts both ways) and demanded the menu entry `__main __optprint`, which R169 moved under SD Card and which therefore could never be hidden - the check now proves the same thing with a real mode-gated entry (Pre-Print hidden while printing). r197 472/0. (4) SPELL LINT: seven domain words the dictionary does not know (`sdm klippy sudo spd macrame re-applies`, each one read in its sentence) are in the whitelist - 1852 strings, 0 suspects, exit 0 again. (5) THE TWO NEW HANDBOOKS: **GENERATORS_TOOLS.pdf** (12 pages - all eleven generators and all fourteen file tools, each with where it sits, what comes out, the real LCD rows read out of `menus/lcd_menues.cfg` so the pages cannot drift, honest limits and one tip) and **CREATIVE_POSSIBILITIES.pdf** (7 pages - twelve recipes that combine the tools: texture x light, MAP relief, litho+frame+stand, labelled texture, brick structure, string art, scale/twist/mirror variations, cake cut, colour + labels, cable/gasket workshop parts, the STL round trip, the checking tools - plus wall-thickness/light tables and a table of what the system deliberately does not do). Battery: macro_render 2093/2093, r81 23/23 (re-frozen, proven), r197 472/0, r142 coverage 142/0 + spell 0 suspects, r166 clean (2733 sections, 406 templates), r232 58/0, r165 clean, r199 18/0. |
| **.142i (R253)** | **The boot halt .142h caused, and three field findings fixed.** (1) BOOT HALT (the printer stopped at 'Can't register R2_DOCTOR as it is an invalid name' -> 'Printer is halted', right after the update): Klipper's gcode.py accepts only UPPER-case names of alnum+underscore where neither the first nor the SECOND character is a digit - 'R2...' parses as a traditional G/M command. The evidence-pack macro is `DOCTOR_PACK` now, the old spelling forwards to it so nothing written against .142h breaks, and a new test checks EVERY macro name in the package against that rule. (2) STL EXPORT ('no object extrusion found' although the file is TYPE=MARKED): the export kept only WALL-OUTER/TOP/BOTTOM/BRIDGE/OVERHANG. Files that name no outer wall (Kiri:Moto 'shell', SuperSlicer 'Perimeter', vase mode) put every wall in WALL-INNER, so the whole object was skipped. The export now relaxes to the inner wall + gap fill when no surface type exists in the file, says so in the message, and the strict outer-skin export is unchanged for normal files. (3) SEGMENT FIX ('already smooth (5372 moves/s) - nothing changed'): the merge chain was capped at 127 micro hops, and the message claimed success at a rate that is 12x over the MCU budget. The cap is now 4096 hops / 5 mm chord (the 0.12 mm chord tube stays the geometry guard), and the message distinguishes 'smooth' from 'still over budget - do not print it'. (4) The post-processor copy `r2_postproc_next.py` is back in step with the stable one. |
| **.142h (R252)** | **Two post-processors and a switch - plus the log hassle is over.** (1) ENGINE SWITCH (user instruction: do not keep rebuilding the post-processor we print with - keep it, copy it, work on the copy, and give me a switch with an instant way back): `tools/r2_postproc_next.py` is a byte-for-byte COPY of `tools/r2_postproc.py` (+ its own header); all new post-processor work happens there from now on and the stable file is not touched. The service still starts the STABLE file - it reads the switch (`save_variables pp_engine`, read from the FILE so it works with klippy down) and hands over via runpy. LCD **System > PP engine: stable / EXPERIMENTAL** (one press, no restart, no sudo, plus an explain row and a `(runs: …)` proof of what is really running). Failure-proof by construction: a missing copy or a syntax error keeps the stable engine, a handover that died is braked for 15 minutes, the diagnostics (`--file-check`, generators, `--stl`) always run on the stable code, and `--engine=stable` on the command line beats the switch. (2) THE EVIDENCE PACK ('Doctor', new `tools/r2_collect.py`): every shutdown round so far began with 'how do I get you the log?'. Now one press on the LCD (**System > Diagnostics > Log pack**) or one SSH line builds ONE file - build stamps, host (RAM/swap/disk/load/top/throttled/dmesg), the three services with journals, the watcher state files, `variables.cfg`, `printer.cfg`, a compact INCIDENT DIGEST cut out of `klippy.log` (every shutdown and probe failure in order, the **worst Reactor stalls with their stacks**, the MCU statistics around each shutdown), the last 3 MB of the raw log, the newest MCU snapshot and the gcodes inventory. It lands in `gcodes/crash_archive/` as `.txt` **and** `.txt.gcode` - because moonraker/Mainsail only LIST files they consider printable, which is exactly why the old `mcu-*.log` crash snapshots sat in that folder and were invisible. The post-processor skips every `R2-DOCTOR-*` name, so the twin can never be printed or tagged. Idle-gated, read-only, no sudo. (3) Docs: MANUAL ch. 14 gets **'The links in the root - how they work, what to watch'** (what a symlink is, where the file really lives, deleting a link vs deleting the file, why Mainsail's list keeps refreshing), ch. 19a2 the log-pack workflow, ch. 19a3 the two-engine switch. Gates: macro_render **2092/2092** (9 new R252 checks incl. the copy being byte-identical to the stable file and the failure-proof handover), r166 OK (2732 sections), r142 142/0, r165 clean, r81 23/23 (version-bump re-freeze, PROVEN: the .142g module from 9efbeb6 reproduces all 23 references byte-exactly and every summary is unchanged), r199 zip check. |
| **.142g (R251)** | **Input shaper without an accelerometer - and the shaper switch is back where the hand is.** (1) E2, the open point: `CALIBRATE_SHAPER` needs `[resonance_tester]` + a sensor, so on this printer those rows can only refuse - while the manual override (`ishaper_ov_*`, survives restarts) had NO menu row at all and was therefore unreachable. New menu **Maintenance > Vibration Cal. > Manual**: a status row that says what Klipper RUNS right now and what the override holds, then per axis `Override: ON/off`, `Shaper` (ZV / MZV / EI / 2HUMP_EI / 3HUMP_EI - or `printer.cfg`) and `Freq` (5-200 Hz, 0.5 Hz steps, pre-filled with the value in use). New macro **SHAPER_MANUAL** behind it: applies live AND stores the boot override, `TYPE=CONFIG` restores the printer.cfg type, frequency AND damping ratio (a restore never silently drops damping) and turns the override off, `ON=0` is override-off only, an unknown type or a frequency outside 5-200 Hz is refused with a message instead of a silent no-op, and on a printer without `[input_shaper]` it says there is nothing to set instead of writing dead variables. (2) **ShpOff is back on the LCD** - R250 deleted Live > Features and with it the only home of the per-feature shaper switch (reported openly in the R250 release report, confirmed by the user). It sits in **Live Tuning > Spd per feat as the row directly below FeatOn** because both act on the feature that prints RIGHT NOW: the label reads `off`, `ON @ <feat>` or `n/a (no shaper)`, and one press calls `SET_FEAT_SHAPER_OFF` for the current feature (or the last stored one when nothing is printing) - no `[input_shaper]`, no row. Every menu row renders on BOTH the reference and the minimal printer (a guardless `printer.input_shaper` in a name template was caught by the compat sweep and fixed). Docs: MANUAL ch. 6 (ShpOff row) / ch. 10.5 (the Manual ladder) / ch. 18 (SHAPER_MANUAL). Gates: macro_render **2080/2080** (9 new assertions: the ShpOff row, the whole `__man` subtree, all SHAPER_MANUAL branches incl. the refusals and the no-`[input_shaper]` case, 23 new menu paths in the tree inventory), r166 OK (2722 sections), r142 142/0, r165 clean, r81 23/23 (version-bump re-freeze, PROVEN: the .142f module reproduces the old references 23/23 and the per-case A/B diff is exactly 3 stamp lines while pr01-pr10 stay byte-identical), r199 zip check. |
| **.142f (R250)** | **The menu the user asked for: four quick rows up front, the rest of the tuning clutter gone.** (1) B1 - the four quick lists (Spd / Acc / PA / Flw per feature) hang DIRECTLY under Live Tuning now, one after the other, no Motion detour: the same sections, only their parent moved (`__live __lmo __vel __spfe` -> `__live __spfe` etc.), so every stored value, every APPLY_TYPE re-apply and every menu test kept working. (2) B2 - **Live > Features is deleted** (175 sections; the user never used it, it survived only as a back door to the hidden ironing flow). | **.142e (R249)** | **Every folder on the LCD, the honest ironing flow, and the quality rule the right way round.** (1) FOLDER ACCESS FROM THE PRINTER (user requirement since .114, the .142d workaround was unusable - files visible, not selectable): the post-processor mirrors every printable file of a subfolder as a SYMLINK in the gcodes root, so Klipper's flat stock `vsdlist` shows the whole library and opens the link directly (up to 200 links, three levels deep, `<subfolder>_<name>.gcode` on a name clash, a real root file always wins, dead links are swept every pass, `.stl.gcode` exports never appear, a user's own symlink outside the root is never touched). `scan()`, the file-check listing and the generator outputs skip links, so nothing is processed twice. `M23` - the stock SD list's file tap - is TAKEN OVER (real: M9923): it selects the file AND opens the start dialog, instead of only selecting and leaving the user stuck. (2) IRONING FLOW = the value that really runs: the feature submenu and both quick rows bind `ov_ironing_flow` (slicer scale 5-100, typical 15-20, default 15) and write it via `SET_OVERRIDE_VALUE NAME=ironing_flow`, which also arms the override - the `feat_flw_ironing` multiplier and its duplicate `__flw` sections are gone (one flow entry per ironing menu, no 100 % default to overwrite). The F flag of the two activators follows `ov_ironing_flow_on`. (3) QUALITY FIX rewritten to the user's rule: a travel keeps retract + Z-hop only when it crosses ink of the SAME layer (layer height measured from the file) or SACRED ink of any layer (outer wall, top, bottom, ironing); everything else drops its armor and prints directly. A crossing that rides naked gets the file's OWN retract (length and F copied from its first retract) and never an invented Z-hop - a file without any retract is reported instead of tampered with. The old rule emptied the ink index at every layer change, which is exactly why the first travel of a layer lost its protection and the surface got scratched. SPEED stays as the user defined it (direct ways, ironing skipped) - no surface rules there on purpose. Docs: MANUAL ch. 2/3.4b/14 (M23, root links, the quality rule, the ironing flow scale). Gates: macro_render **2067/2067**, r166 OK (2873 sections), r142 142/0, r165 clean, r81 23/23 (stamp-only re-freeze, PROVEN: the same module with the .142d stamp strings reproduces all 23 references byte-identical), r199 zip 18/0. |
| **.142d (R248)** | **The LCD folder browser is parked - and with it the biggest unnecessary cost in the menu.** Measured in the Klipper source first: `MenuList.draw_container` renders only the VISIBLE rows per frame, so a dynamic name in a closed menu costs exactly nothing - the field's 'MCU at 60-70%' diagnosis does not hold (`mcu_awake=0.012`, i.e. the MCU slept 98.8% of the crash window). What DID hold was the 0.677 s reactor block at print start, and the folder browser was the one part of the menus that talked to klippy on its own: a recursive tree walk on every watcher pass, a `gcode_macro _SDM` query per cycle, and multi-KB `sd_cur`/`sd_view` pushes. PARKED, NOT DELETED: SD Card is Klipper's own `vsdlist` again (G-Codes root, stock Start/Resume/Pause/Cancel - the four kill-overrides are gone with the browser), with **Optimized** (40 slots) and **Generated** (20 slots) as submenus, because those two folders are what a print actually comes from. Everything the browser needs stays in the code behind ONE switch (`SD_BROWSER_ON = False` in the watcher, plus `enable: {False}` on each of its 47 menu rows): set the switch, drop those lines and it is back. Nested folders, deleting and paging = Mainsail; the generators do not care where the display looks. LIVE-PROVEN (fake moonraker on 7125): with the switch off the watcher makes ZERO `_SDM` queries and ZERO `sd_*` pushes. THE FOUR QUICK MENUS found the same treatment on the display side: each opens with a head row (**== SPEED ==**, **== ACCEL ==**, **== PA ==**, **== FLOW ==**) so the flow you are in is written on the screen, and the duplicate 'value of the feature that happens to be printing' rows (FeatSpd / FeatAcc) are GONE - that setting sits in that feature's own row of the list, one place per setting. Globals stay on top where they were. The features menu, the audiovisual feedback and the UART stay untouched by explicit request. Version .142d/R248, r81 re-frozen (stamp class, proofs in the check header). |
| **.142c (R246d)** | **The start window: motion-blindness closed.** .142b killed the flood - the 04:04 field log proves it (0 push lines, reactor stalls down from 10 s to 0.07 s) - but the print start STILL died with 'Timer too close', now in a reactor window of just 0.68 s that carried an LCD button dispatch, TMC stepper-enable callbacks AND our own status queries. The blind spot: a manual preheat + G28/homing runs with _STATE mode 'idle' AND print_stats 'idle' - every busy check the watcher had was blind exactly there, so it kept its full per-pass activity (3 status probes + info + the _SDM read every ~6 s) and even pushed 2 KB _PREVIEW_SET scripts straight into the homing window. THE FIX: klipper's idle_timeout ('Printing' from the first move until the idle timeout expires) now rides BOTH busy probes in the SAME query (zero extra requests) - motion IS busy, whatever the mode system says; the macros already read this signal, the watcher now does too. Preview pushes defer while anything moves (a waiting start dialog without motion still gets its file info immediately) and flush once the printer is idle. While the printer moves: no tree walk, no _SDM reads, no pushes, no preview scripts - the pass sleeps 12 s and does nothing but the recovery tick. CAUGHT IN REVIEW BEFORE RELEASE: idle_timeout alone would have been a trap - with the configured timeout: 3600 it stays 'Printing' for up to an HOUR after the last move (the watcher would have gone silent for an hour after any preheat wiggle); the truth is toolhead.print_time, frozen = drained queue. PLUS the external root-cause analysis round (its recommendations, our code): SET_LED_STATE is a no-op on an UNCHANGED state now (FORCE=1 re-applies; the four deliberate re-apply callers carry it) - every state change used to rewrite the whole neopixel chain (STOP_LED_EFFECTS + effect + nozzle + bedlight), reactor time AND a failed-update retry chance each time; the _AV start timers (LED+beep 1.5 s after homing/print start - deep inside the move window) now wait for a DRAINED queue (print_time frozen between checks; the heating window is the natural gap; print AV gives up silently after 120 s); the duplicate scan ADMITS hidepid blindness (root strays invisible to /proc scans AND plain pgrep - the persistent field report of sudo-only visible twins) instead of implying a clean scan. Mainsail/webcam polling and the Neopixel wiring stay hardware-side advice (report). Version .142c/R246d, r81 re-frozen (stamp class, proofs in the check header). |
| **.142b (R246c)** | **The push flood that killed prints mid-job - found, fixed, regression-guarded.** Night session Nov 10->11: a 20-minute frame print (20 layers, KAMP) ran from 23:53 while the console FLOODED with the full _SDM mirror + image list every ~5 s - through waiting, through printing, even through the shutdown state - until the MCU died at 00:08:21 with 'Timer too close' (124x in the mcu log, FIRMWARE_RESTART 00:08:38). Root cause chain, fully proven: (1) the one _SDM status query passed its object list through the HOST parameter of the moonraker call - the query could never succeed, the code always fell back to stale .141 file values, every change-compare said 'different' and the push loop never stopped; (2) the push gate's busy probe returns 'not busy' when the Pi cannot answer (by design - it must never block the watcher itself), and a Pi drowning in the flood's own API+echo load stops answering: the flood held the gate open for itself; (3) the image-list push had NO busy guard at all. FOUR fixes: the status query now passes objs= as the keyword it always should have been; both push paths demand CONFIRMED idle (a new strict gate counts an unanswered probe as BUSY - fail-safe direction flipped for pushes); failed mirror pushes back off (3 fails -> 4 silent cycles) and a push times out at 20 s / 1 retry instead of hanging; SDNAV_GO refuses unknown paths and self-heals to the deepest valid level (the 'optimized/optimized/...' x7 path-doubling from the 23:35 log is dead). And the harness debt: the render battery's PASS/FAIL loop sat mid-file - every test appended after it (R214-R238, 10 of them) was silently never evaluated. The loop now runs at the end; all 10 stale tests were triaged against the current code (features intact - tests corrected, among them one whose expectation contradicted its own sibling test) and the battery counts honest again: 2033/2033. Version .142b/R246c. |
| **.142a (R246b)** | **Field-log hotfix: the download button was a memory bomb.** First .142 field evening (Nov 10, 21:46): starting the frame print after homing died with `MCU 'mcu' shutdown: Missed scheduling of next digital out event` — and around that same moment the user clicked the WebUI's klippy.log export: that endpoint read the WHOLE klippy.log into RAM (20 MB+ by now — one full config dump per restart, and the day had many), on a Pi that was already deep in swap. The host froze completely (both web UIs dead, LCD dead), the stalled host stopped scheduling the probe-enable pin's digital-out events during homing -> shutdown. The auto-recovery brought the printer back after 178 s (second production save). THREE fixes: (1) klippylog export reads only the LAST 6 MB (section filter drops the partial first line anyway — the evidence sits at the end); (2) the crash-snapshot export reads the LAST 3 MB instead of the first — both snapshots the user downloaded were byte-IDENTICAL (same md5!) because the old cap kept the file's history prefix while the crash evidence lives at the end; (3) the LCD mirror refill: after the recovery's FIRMWARE_RESTART the SD browser showed `page 1 of 1 (0 entries in /)` forever — the _SDM mirror is RAM and died with klippy while the daemon's push caches still claimed 'already pushed'. The watcher now detects the empty live mirror (one flag in the existing objects query), drops every mirror cache and refills within a pass. Version .142a/R246b, r81 re-frozen (stamp class). |
| **.142 (R246)** | **Shutdown-Endbekämpfung: the LCD mirror moves to RAM.** The user traced the Nov 10 shutdown trio (18:14 print end, 18:57 file selection, 19:52 print-start click - all on files that always printed fine) to the HOST: 49 MB free, deep in swap, and every one of those moments sat on a synchronous SAVE_VARIABLE that rewrites the WHOLE variables.cfg inside klippy's reactor - and ours carried the rebuildable giants (sd_tree ~200 entries, sd_view, gen_files, opt_list, litho_*). THE FIX (user-ordered architecture change): all mirror data now lives in a new `_SDM` gcode_macro (SET_GCODE_VARIABLE - zero disk writes, zero file serialization in the reactor; the watcher reads path/page LIVE from the object via one moonraker query, file only as fallback); variables.cfg shrinks to true persistence (profiles, toggles, settings). Plus the round's payload: crash_archive disappears from the LCD SD browser (empty-forensics folder trapped the field navigation) and gets a WebUI 'crash' button next to klippy.log (newest mcu-*.log snapshot as one-click .txt, served by a new r2_menu endpoint); ambient LED frame around the WebUI page showing roughly the printer's LED mood (poll-lagged on purpose, never in sync); the SD index load is now VISIBLE ('SD index ready: N entries in X.Xs', one line per tree change); THE PAGING CLASS FIX: klipper's menu.py re-evaluates enable templates only when a container is (re)entered - name templates render live every frame. All 27 texture-pattern slots (enable-frozen per page) became 8 rows that slice the list live via the new `_TX_PATTERN` macro (page math + save + the 27 info texts in one place), and the 6 litho page-turner enable lines are gone (their names already showed live state). Regression-guarded: the render battery now fails on ANY enable line bound to a *_page variable. Version .142/R246, menu export re-embedded (2442 sections, sha 57fcac04b14e43b2), r81 re-frozen (stamp class). |
| **.141 (R245)** | **Park first, celebrate later - the PRINT_END reorder.** .140 field log (Nov 10, 18:14): the 1h15m texture print FINISHED, and one second after PRINT_DONE the MCU died of 'Timer too close' - the r2_done melody broke off after its first tone and the nozzle was still resting on the part (user report, and the auto-recovery of .140 brought the printer back after 100 s - first production save). Root cause, twofold: (1) PRINT_END ran stats -> LED -> melody -> fan -> heaters -> cleanup and parked the head LAST - the nozzle sat on the finished object through the whole end choreography, exactly what the user had forbidden before; (2) the FIRST action of that window was _OPT_END_CAPTURE's SAVE_VARIABLEs - synchronous variables.cfg SD writes in klippy's reactor (the R216b stall class, up to 1.9 s per write) while the file's last moves were still draining. NEW ORDER: safety restores (in-memory) -> state cleanup (in-memory, R160b keeps its refused-move protection) -> PRESENTATION MOVE (head up + aside) -> stats -> opt capture -> LED -> melody -> fan/heaters. The queue runs empty at the park move, so every later reactor stall (SD writes included) has no moves left to starve; the done-sound now only plays for a PRESENTED part. Regression caught in review: _PRINT_CLEANUP resets s.layer - the stats capture the layer count before the cleanup now (the R174 'layers: 0' bug stays dead). Version .141/R245; r81 re-frozen (stamp class). |
| **.140 (R244)** | **MCU-shutdown auto-recovery - the watcher heals the printer.** .139 field log (Nov 10 15:12): another `MCU 'mcu' shutdown: Timer too close` at homing end after selecting a 14746-moves/s texture file (the flagged SegFix class - the system had warned 'MCU risk - SegFix!' at selection). User request: "if I can always restart via the Klipper UI, you must be able to see the MCU shutdown too - reset the firmware first, then reboot the whole system." Klippy cannot heal itself and moonraker never restarts a shutdown klippy - but the WATCHER is an independent process holding the same moonraker API the UI buttons use. NEW in r2_postproc (`_recovery_tick`, one cheap /printer/info per cycle): on shutdown state the ladder runs L1 FIRMWARE_RESTART (10 s settle, 2 tries) -> L2 klipper service restart (90 s) -> L3 full Pi reboot via moonraker (210 s dead, or immediately on the 3rd episode within 15 min; max 1/hour, persisted in watcher_recovery.json so a reboot never resets its own brake). ONLY 'Timer too close'/communication-loss/missed-scheduling classes auto-recover - heater/thermal/config shutdowns stay down on purpose (they need eyes, not a restart loop). klippy.log is snapshotted to gcodes/crash_archive/ BEFORE the first restart (forensics survive every rotation, capped at 7). Switch: `SET_RECOVERY MODE=on/off`, LCD System > MCU Recovery, default on - read from the variables FILE because no macro can run while klippy is dead. Version .140/R244, r197 menu pin 2441 -> 2442 (export re-embedded), new r244_field_check battery incl. a full state-machine simulation with a faked moonraker. |
| **.139 (R243)** | **The LED ready-crash - .138 never became ready, printer dead in the field.** .138 field log: klippy died at EVERY startup with `Internal error during ready callback: division by zero` (first traceback 111-123; only two `Start printer` lines - no restart loop, klippy stayed in shutdown state). Frame: led_effect's layerCylon builds each sweep as `frames = int(duration * frame_rate)` and interpolates `pct = frame / (frames - 1)` - the ONLY such division in the library. R242's diet dropped the Dauerlauf rates 6 -> 3 fps, which shrank r2_leveling's `cylon 0.6` from 3 frames to **one** -> pct = 0/0 -> ZeroDivisionError in the ready callback -> every boot dead. The 5-6 s console 'loop' was NOT a klippy restart loop: the postproc daemon retries its sd_tree/gen_files boot push every poll while klippy refuses SAVE_VARIABLEs (by design, 'push failed - retrying next cycle') - it heals itself the moment klippy is ready. FIX (config only, the daemon is untouched): both cylon layers (r2_homing 0.8 s, r2_leveling 0.6 s) -> **1.0 s = 3 frames at 3 fps** (above the crash line AND a visible sweep); the .138 serial diet stays intact (idle/waiting/heating/homing/leveling/pid 3 fps, boot 6, printing 5). NEW GUARD r243_field_check.py: simulates the frame math for EVERY layer of EVERY [led_effect] in EVERY cfg (A1 `int(duration*rate)>=2`, moving layers `>=3`), A2 reproduces the exact field crash (asserts `cylon 0.6 @ 3` WOULD raise), A3 pins the rate table, V1 pins version .139 everywhere. Version bumps: state cfg, POSTPROC_RELEASE/BUILD (`R2-2026-11-09.139 (R243)`), WebUI badge + page ver R243, manual, test guide ch. 28; r81 re-frozen (stamp class, A/B-proven). CI: r243 15/0 (new), r242 20/0, r240 29/0, macro_render 2027/2027, r81 23/23, full tier 28/28. |
| **.138 (R242)** | **The start-window diet - 'Timer too close' during homing, solved on the software side.** .137 field log: two `MCU 'mcu' shutdown: Timer too close` right in the homing phase after file selection, on CLEAN files (MAXRATE 12, gcodein=0) - the klippy log showed the reactor stalled 0.541 s (`Reactor busy`), built from our own start-window loads. (1) DOUBLE PREVIEW PUSH: every selection bumped preview_nonce twice by design (R62 selection + R195 dialog re-peek 8 s later), shipping the ~20 KB script (two EXCLUDE_OBJECT_DEFINE polygons + raw _FILE_INFO) through moonraker->webhooks->klippy TWICE while homing plans just-in-time moves - now the re-peek is SKIPPED when it cannot change anything (same file + mtime + size, previous push ACKed, within 120 s, and klippy still holds the values - `_PREVIEW.name` is the liveness proof; a restart resets it and the push re-fires). (2) FAT WEB POLL: the page polled ~17 objects every 3 s incl. save_variables, which klippy serializes as the WHOLE variables dict (sd_tree, gen_files, profiles, depot - 10-30 KB JSON per poll); r2_menu now tiers save_variables to 10 s AND diets itself during a start: a tiny precheck (print_stats + _STATE) detects waiting/starting and queries only the light objects (temps, mode, progress), merging the rest from the last full snapshot - LCD-started prints are covered too (the R215 page quiet only armed on the page's own commands). (3) LED FRAMES: the animated effects ran 6 fps = six neopixel transactions/s on a link that already drops updates at idle ('Neopixel update did not succeed', each retried by klippy = reactor churn); Dauerlauf effects now 3 fps (boot stays 6, printing stays 5 - r164 guards it). (4) _WATCHDOG re-armed _STATE_WATCHER at 0.5 s every 5 s making it tick ~20% faster than designed - now consistent 1 s. NOT software: the log also shows bytes_retransmit (lost serial bytes with cold heaters) and one Pi reboot - TESTANLEITUNG ch. 27 carries the hardware checklist (USB cable, Pi power/throttling, LED data resistor) + the segment-fix reminder for old texture files (the OTHER Timer-too-close class, V04 = 6000 moves/s the day before). CI: r242_field_check 14/0 (new), r240 29/0, r164/r235 green, macro_render 2027/2027, megatest 190/0, r197 471/0, tier 28/28, r81 23/23 (stamp-class re-freeze, A/B-proven). |
| **.137 (R241)** | **The clean version number.** Pure renumber of .136aj - same code, same features, nothing functional changed. Why: the letter-suffix chain (.136a ... .136aj) was confusing in the field ("which version is the current one?"), so the round starts a bare number. The update odometer orders it natively: **137 > 136aj** (the number dominates any letter suffix, `sfx('')=0` is a first-class bare release) - an update from .136ai OR .136aj just works, no --force. What did change: version strings (state, postproc core stamp `R2-2026-11-09.137 (R241)`, manual, PDF scripts), the WebUI version badge repaired (.136ab -> .137 - it had been stale since the .136ab round; page state ver R241), and four batteries taught that a version may carry NO letter suffix (r227 F3 / r234 predecessor math `.137 -> .136z` / r217 C4 / r218 badge pin - single-letter regex leftovers from before the two-letter era R229, two of them silently red for months). r81 mr01-mr13 re-frozen (core-stamp class only, A/B-proven against the .136aj module: byte-diff = the stamp lines). Field check: TESTANLEITUNG ch. 26 (ch. 25 covers the R240 features - they all ship here unchanged). CI: r240 29/0, r217 15/15 + r218 25/25 (both repaired), r227/r234 green, macro_render 2027/2027, megatest 190/0, r197 471/0, full tier 28/28. |
| **.136aj (R240)** | **The start-blocker fix + the profile master round.** (1) START BLOCKER SOLVED (.136ai field log): 6x `Malformed command '_M204_SET VALUE=8000'` right after the prime line killed every print start - klipper's dispatcher builds command names from [A-Z_] ONLY (digits END a name, klippy/gcode.py args_r), so our own R167 macro name `_M204_SET` parsed as cmd `_M204` + rawparams `_SET VALUE=8000`; the R237 `_M204` alias finally dispatched it and shlex raised the fatal error (6 echoes = 6 macro nesting levels; before R237 the same line was a harmless 'Unknown command'). Fix: `_ACCEL_SET` (digit-free), alias removed, both tools rewrite `_M204_SET VALUE=x` and `_M204 ...` lines to M204, and the new r240 battery SIMULATES the klipper tokenizer over every macro name and every dispatched menu/macro line - this bug class can never ship again. (2) PROFILES - ONE Depot, one profile per file (master prompt): `SAVE/LOAD/DELETE_DEPOT_PRESET` + `DEPOT_INFO` store EVERY Pre-Print value - temps, retraction (length/retract/unretract), PA + smooth time, accel, velocity, min-cruise + square-corner, global flow, fan incl. min/max/bridge/clamp, ironing flow, all 25 per-feature slots (speed/accel/flow/PA), shaper-off, first-layer speed AND now Z-hop (on + height) + the extruder drive - values AND switches, ON and OFF alike; the 8 default slots + the material mini-presets retired from the LCD (console legacy stays), material quick templates live in Pre-Print > Material (load/save/reset per material). Max ONE 'pre' profile per file (double-press save/load/delete via the gen_arm idiom - first press arms, second runs, the row jumps back so the display shows the result). (3) THE OPTIMIZED FLOW (master prompt): ranking YES now ALSO registers the copy's tuned values as its OWN pre-print profile (the neutralized replay set - percents live in the file, absolutes stay) - selecting the optimized copy later AUTO-LOADS it with a confirmation beep; the ORIGINAL keeps its own profile: before AND after both exist, nothing is lost, nothing is auto-saved without a YES. (4) IRONING quick value: the stiff 100% SPEED row became the real IRONING FLOW (default 15%, range 5-100 - typical 15-20%) in Live > Motion > Velocity > Spd/feat; all four ironing-flow rows default 15 now. (5) LIVE mirrors PRE-PRINT MOTION: the four quick lists (Spd/Acc/PA/Flw per feature) moved from the Live top level INTO Live > Motion (Velocity gets Spd/Acc, Extrusion gets PA/Flw) - exactly the Pre-Print Motion structure; the 32 feature activator rows moved from File info INTO their feature submenus (Pre-Print gated by file types, Live always clickable with (parked) names per R174b); Z-hop stays directly above the babysteps. (6) PER-JOB LED PROGRESS BAR: while a watcher job works (generation, texture, segment fix, optimized copy) the status LEDs show a green bar for THAT job - restarts at 0 for the next one (a whole-queue bar would lie when jobs get added mid-run); `_LED_JOB_PROGRESS PCT=` + `_LED_JOB_DONE` restore the state LEDs; throttled to ~1 push/1.2s, respects the LEDs master switch + the 0.5 power cap. (7) The queue Stop row REALLY double-presses now (the R238 row called the macro without CONFIRM - only the console could stop; fixed with the arm idiom). CI: r240_field_check 29/0 (incl. the klipper-tokenizer simulator catching the old blocker as regression proof), macro_render 2027/2027, megatest 190/0, r197 471/0 (pin 2441, web re-embedded), r142 139/0 + r143 68/0, r234/r235/r236/r237/r238/r239 green, r81 23/23 + A/B double-proof (segfix/texture byte-identical), tier 28/28. |
| **.136ai (R238+R239)** | **The master-prompt round: auto safety, queue control, textures without stripes - and the start blocker.** (1) Generated files are MCU-safe BY CONSTRUCTION: the job worker measures every fresh output's worst move rate and runs the segment fix AUTOMATICALLY above 400 moves/s (the .136ae log: a texture printed at 6000 moves/s) - '[queue] ... AUTO segment fix - N micro move(s) merged, rate -> R/s', no separate menu step; the manual fix stays for old uploads. (2) Queue CONTROL, not just a count: Skip ends the RUNNING job (the next starts), Stop ends it AND clears the queue - the cancel rides a nonce channel into _job_tick (a safe point inside every generator hot loop), the partial output file is deleted, a job parked in the busy/RAM wait is dropped before it starts; the row moved to '__queue' = DIRECTLY above the SD card. (3) STL exports become downloadable: STL folder + name.stl.gcode (Mainsail lists it, one click downloads, strip the .gcode ending) - the watcher treats them as DATA (never parsed, retagged or meta-finalized; _is_gen_name refuses them), the LCD browser click explains instead of printing. (4) TEXTURE rework per the master prompt: AUTO TILT - a helical shift of m integer cells over the height angles every pattern ~2 deg off the layer plane (seam stays perfect: the wrap differs by exactly k integer cells; the top closes: integer total shift) - directional looks keep their own angle (BASKET 30 deg weave, KNIT 3, HILBERT 4; ROPE/FLUTED/BARK/FACETS/MACRAME/PLANKS/MAP self-aligned), reported as '[auto-tilt 2.0 deg]'; 6 NEW patterns (KNIT nested V-loops, FLUTED vertical flutes, BARK vertical cracks, SCALES domed shingles, FACETS diamond relief, MACRAME crossing cords - 27 total, page 4); PLANKS now run VERTICAL (lengthwise); MAP detects the background (Otsu + border vote) and treats it as ALPHA - zero displacement, feathered edges (layer adhesion survives) - plus ONCE mode: one aspect-true wrap positioned TOP/CENTER/BOTTOM (the masked background hides the seam) next to the endless TILE wrap; the menu separates FUZZY from BUMPS parameters (enable-gated rows). (5) R239 - 'the print will not start' (field log klippy-3h 2): 5x Malformed command 'EXCLUDE_OBJECT_DEFINE ... POLYGON=[[..], [..]]' - the MIRROR tool re-emitted the object polygon with ', ' separators (R224's join), Klipper's shlex splits values at whitespace, the line dies as malformed, klippy lands in the error state and every start of such a file (and every selection replay!) fails; the texture files inherited the line verbatim. Fixed THREE ways: the mirror emitter joins compact (the R87.2 rule), every rewriter HEALS spaced polygons while streaming (segment fix, texture, the selection replay, range excerpts), and the segment fix got EFFECTIVE for the spiral-vase chord flood: Z-carrying G1s now merge (the old regex was blind to them) and the old 0.35mm total cap is replaced by chains of <=128 moves bounded by CHORD DEVIATION <= 0.12mm (a fifth of the line width - flat floods merge freely, wandering paths stop; the field rose: 18000 moves/s stayed 18000, merged=113 of half a million). Also: the WebUI temperature flood was audited - NO sender in the package (M105/M155 unused, gcodein=0 across the whole first log); classic cause is an external poller (leftover OctoPrint service), console messages never loaded the MCU (R235 proof). CI: r238_field_check 26/0 + r239_field_check 14/0 (shlex proof spaced-vs-compact, mirror emitter E2E, heal in segfix/texture/replay, spiral bomb >400 -> <400 with Z+E preserved, wandering path NOT collapsed, 128 cap, r142 bomb compat), macro_render 2026/2026, r142 139/0 + r143 68/0 (kill channel = documented live exception), megatest 190/0, r197 471/0 (pin 2495, web re-embedded), r81 23/23 (segment fix never touches make_range), r236/r235/r234/r232 green, tier 28/28. |
| **.136ah (R237)** | **MAP images finally load + the _M204 ghost is caught.** (1) 'Texture cannot load images as MAP' was THREE breaks working together: a ROOT image (the Mainsail drag+drop standard) never set the pick - the SD browser only talked ('use Tools > Lithophane') while only SUBFOLDER clicks picked; the watcher would have died on SameFileError copying a root pick onto itself; and texture_map is a RELATIVE name that the script resolved against the SOURCE file's folder (broken for sources from Texture/, Labels/ ...). Root clicks pick now (the watcher skips the self-copy and confirms 'ready for Texture MAP'), the watcher hands the texture an ABSOLUTE map path (subfolder pick first, then its root copy), the script itself tries the literal relative and then the basename next to the source (a missing image names every tried path), and TEXTURE_APPLY fails EARLY ('MAP needs an image') instead of dying in the queue. (2) 'Unknown command:"_M204"' twice per print: the field log proves the calls fire FROM THE FILE (virtual SD context, start block right behind the thumbnails, right after PRINT_START's post-heat PA/velocity settings, gcodein=0) - no sender exists in the package (only _M204_SET, proven by git log -S over the whole history) nor in the user's printer_data configs (a silent grep IS the no-match answer); the lines ride in from the slicer profile's start G-code. Three answers: the _M204 alias forwards the raw parameter text into the M204 interceptor (feature accel, global overwrite, live cap, honest tracker - no more Unknown command, even for files never touched), r2_texture and the segment fix REWRITE legacy _M204 lines to M204 while processing in place (done message carries '[legacy _M204 renamed: N]'; _M204_SET stays untouched - and the segment fix's early-exit path no longer throws rewrites away), and the generators never emitted it. | **.136aa (R229)** | **The vocabulary REST LIST - the last open items of the slicer-family compilation (user question from the .136v audit round).** (1) *Non-extrusion annotations are inert*: KISSlicer marks retract/travel moves as `; feature Destring` and the seam point as `; feature Seam`; G-code viewers show travel/retract/tool-change/color-change/pause events as pseudo types. A seam sits INSIDE a wall loop - as a feature block it would type the following WALL extrusions TRAVEL/CUSTOM and the wall overrides would silently stop applying there. The TYPE regex now rejects these words (whole-line anchored, near-misses like 'wipe wall' unaffected): the feature context persists through them. Direct `_canon_type` callers get a map safety net (all -> TRAVEL, the established no-extrusion canon). (2) *Purge/prime structures are one class*: prime wall, wipe wall (CORRECTION: skirt since R225 - a wipe wall IS a purge structure), prime pillar, wiping tower, purge block, purge line, prime blob -> PRIME-TOWER. (3) *Rest assignments*: belt lead -> skirt (belt-printer adhesion strip), brim ears -> brim, painted support -> support, the raft stack -> bottom surface (the existing 'raft' approximation). (4) *Infill pattern names* (tpms/tpms-d/tpms-fk, octet, tetrahedral, stars, lateral honeycomb, lattice, spiral inset, tri-hexagon) are patterns, not features - all reduce to sparse infill (R216 stage-7 rule). Field context: .136z confirmed working in the field (clean boot log, no spam/storm/banner - the export shows a boring healthy boot). | **.136z (R228)** | **The .136y field log: the Orca vase printed (R226 works!), but layer 1 became a pause storm that ended in an MCU shutdown.** Field evidence: full-bed mesh with the CENTER-only hint, probe-safe prime line, 20x20mm park box - all R226 degradations visible and working; then quick-pause/resume churn on layer 1, "State re-synced: mode was stuck in 'paused'" after EVERY resume, third resume -> MCU 'Timer too close' shutdown, and every subsequent reconcile push re-printed the whole shutdown banner (2x per 6 s). The user asked whether the motion sensor's start chirps ('nothing conveyed') armed the pauses - in THIS log they did not (the pause reason was 'quick pause' = the LCD item), but the chirps are real and now harmless. (1) *Watcher resume race*: after our RESUME the mode flips to 'printing' a moment before print_stats follows (M24 updates it only as the file read resumes - seconds on a slow first-layer file); the watcher's bypassed-pause branch sampled that gap and knocked the mode back to 'paused' (the print kept running, the desync heal needed ~10 s - "stuck in paused" after every resume). The branch now needs THREE consecutive ticks: a pause that bypassed us (Mainsail button) persists, the resume gap never does. (2) *Filament sensor grace windows* (the user's question): _PRIME_LINE arms 30 s at print start, _RESUME_COMMON arms 20 s after the refill moves - _FILAMENT_RUNOUT reports chatter instead of arming a pause while the motion wheel lags behind the building pressure; a real runout re-fires after the window. (3) *Pause NOW arm expiry*: the two-press guard had NO timeout - one armed click stayed armed forever and any later single click (or an EMI ghost enter on the 12864 encoder) paused the print; the arm expires after 5 s. (4) *Banner storm + burst*: the daemon reconcile is rate-limited to one attempt per 10 s (the print-start backlog scan amplified 8 pushes in 8 s) and gated on klippy_state=='ready' - no push into a shutdown klippy, where each one re-printed the full 'Timer too close' banner to every client. (2) *The day export that ran old code*: the field's 78-byte klippy-day.txt was byte-identical to the .136w bug - because web/r2_menu.py is loaded at MOONRAKER startup and update.sh restarted klipper + watcher but not moonraker; the export ran the old filter until the PI was rebooted by hand (the 3h export after the reboot came out perfect: 1871 lines, dumps skipped). update.sh now restarts moonraker itself (after the census - that talks TO moonraker). The .136p mkPrinter unwrap was a no-op against the klipper truth (gcode_macro get_status returns self.variables FLAT - verified against klipper source). The REAL fix: the component now queries in TIERS without a default (errors raise): (1) field-list batch, (2) all-None batch (older klippy may reject field lists; configfile rides a 60 s settings-keys cache so the megabytes transit at most once a minute), (3) per-object; an empty result answers ok:False WITH the reason - the page never fakes a printer (badge: 'no data: <reason>', dashboard chip NO LIVE DATA). The r197 fakes now mirror the REAL moonraker semantics (default-swallow vs raise) - the R211 'fake richer than reality' lesson, now with the API contract read from the moonraker source. (2) *The travel golden rule*: no travel may ever CROSS freshly printed plastic - a spatial hash of the current layer's ink answers per travel (endpoint touches and parallel neighbor lines never count), retract+Z-hop only where it is really needed, no matter how short the jump. (3) *Bed screws, Mainsail's flow on the LCD* (Maintenance > Bed Screws): Start homes and moves to screw 1, Next walks screw by screw with clock notation (01:20 = 1 turn + 20 minutes, CW/CCW), the display shows the measured deviation of the current position, :ok when a screw sits, Round starts a fresh pass, Done reports the max deviation. (5) *Geometry generator* (Generator > Geometry): TRIANGLE/SQUARE/POLY 3-12/CYLINDER, walls 1-8 or wall thickness in mm, infill 0-100 %, WALLS=1 = vase mode (spiral, no top); output V01_Shape_... in Shapes/. (9) *Two new file fixes* (Pre-Print > Fixes): **Quality** applies the travel golden rule to a sliced file - retract/hop blocks around travels that cross NO ink drop (the feature stage contributes ONLY the travel detection, no feature is re-tagged), crossings keep their armor, naked crossings are named; **Speed** merges consecutive travels into direct ways (position-true) and skips the ironing pass (G92 stitch keeps absolute-E files exact). | **.136h (R209)** | **THE UR-BUG, found at last: the web-LCD page's JavaScript had NEVER executed - not once, in any browser, since .136.** The .136g field log: page loads ('die seite wird geladen, aber ich kann da immer noch nichts machen ... es steht auch nur main_ bei menuebaum ... beim klicken passiert nix'). Root cause chain (all three found by the NEW integration test T8, which parses and runs the REAL shipped page in node against the REAL component response): (1) **SYNTAX ERROR SINCE R201** - the JSON embed replaced only the /*EMBEDDED_MENU_JSON*/ marker and left the placeholder's trailing ' null;' in place: 'const EMBEDDED_MENU_JSON = {...} null;' does not parse, so the whole script block died silently in every browser - no menu render (breadcrumb stuck at the static '__main'), no click handlers, no fallback, nothing. Masked for 9 releases by the 403 layers on top (page unreadable, then POSTs rejected) - and by the test pyramid itself: every web test checked its own slice (evaluator functions vs jinja, JSON presence, HTTP status codes) - NOBODY EVER PARSED THE WHOLE SHIPPED SCRIPT. (2) Moonraker's envelope: every endpoint answer ships as {"result": ...} - the page read it raw, so even with a parsing script DOC.tree would have been undefined. (3) pollLive() was called synchronously at script end, BEFORE loadDoc()'s fetch resolved (LIVE still false) - live values would have NEVER polled. FIXES: embed hardened in BOTH branches (the splice branch eats the stray placeholder 'null'), the page unwraps the result envelope + shape-checks before use, pollLive starts after a successful loadDoc. T8 now runs the complete page script (DOM/fetch stubs, envelope included) against the real component response AND the embedded fallback - menu renders (13 items in the runner), badge goes 'Werte live', tree-down falls back to the embedded export. | **.136g (R208)** | **FIELD FIX - 'schon beim offnen der Seite' 403, auch inkognito: nginx could not READ the page.** The .136f field diagnosis (run BY the user, verbatim output): `sudo -u www-data head ... web/index.html` -> 'Permission denied'. nginx runs as www-data; a static file it cannot read is answered with 403 Forbidden - ALREADY ON OPENING THE PAGE, on every browser, cache or not, IP or hostname. The serve path ran through /home/pi - home-dir traverse permissions can flip on ANY update (Raspberry Pi OS tightens, an unpack lands oddly, whatever flipped here). The nasty part: the API proxy paths kept working (moonraker answered everything) - which is exactly why the R207 self-test, the E2E and every proxy check were green while the page would not even load: NOBODY tested the static page itself. FIXES: (1) the page is served from WEB_ROOT (/var/www/r2lcd) now - world-readable, OUTSIDE every home directory, the standard place for web roots; install_web.sh copies index.html there (sudo, 644/755) and --update-only re-syncs it on every package update (R2_WEB_ROOT override for tests). (2) the installer self-test GETs THE PAGE ITSELF now (must answer 200) in addition to the guard POSTs - an unreadable serve path can never pass silently again; on failure it names the exact check commands (ls -la, sudo -u www-data head). (3) --status reports the page location; --uninstall removes it. E2E with real nginx 1.26 AND a 750-mode home dir (www-data locked out, the old alias proven 403): page 200, live 200, same-origin command 200, foreign origin 403 at the proxy, refresh restores a stale page, self-test retry path exercised. Field-bug class #15: serve-path file permissions - static, outside every code test, visible only to a self-test that requests the actual page.
